<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the standard sine map of the two-dimensional torus has positive metric entropy with respect to normalized area for every sufficiently large positive parameter. This gives a full parameter tail, and hence answers Sinai's positive-parameter-measure conjecture affirmatively.

- **C01** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-entropy)): There exists $k_0>0$ such that $$h_m(f_k)>0\qquad\text{for every }k\ge k_0.$$ Equivalently, for every such $k$ the largest Lyapunov exponent of $f_k$ is positive on a set of positive $m$-measure.
  - Explanation: Positive metric entropy for normalized area holds throughout a sufficiently large positive parameter tail; positivity need only occur on a set of positive area.
- **C02** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-components)): For each $k\ge k_0$ there is a measurable $f_k$-invariant set $E=E(k)$ with $m(E)>0$ such that $(E,m(E)^{-1}m\|_E,f_k)$ is ergodic and $f_k$ has one positive and one negative Lyapunov exponent almost everywhere on $E$. For some integer $N=N(k,E)\ge1$, there is a measurable partition $E=E_0\sqcup\cdots\sqcup E_{N-1}$ such that $f_k(E_j)=E_{j+1\bmod N}$ and $(E_j,m(E_j)^{-1}m\|_{E_j},f_k^N)$ is Bernoulli for every $j$. All invariance and partition statements are modulo $m$-null sets; each $E_j$ has area $m(E)/N>0$.
  - Explanation: There is a positive-area nonuniformly hyperbolic ergodic component with a finite cyclic Bernoulli decomposition.
- **C03** (bound, [source](#stmt-setup-shortfall-count)): Let $z_0,\ldots,z_N$ be real numbers with $z_j-z_{j-1}\le1$, and set $$\mathcal D=N-(z_N-z_0) =\sum_{j=1}^N\bigl(1-z_j+z_{j-1}\bigr).$$ For $c<1$, the number of indices $j$ for which some $0\le l<j$ satisfies $z_j-z_l<c(j-l)$ is at most $C\mathcal D/(1-c)$, with an absolute constant $C$. The same conclusion holds for the number of indices $j$ with some $j<l\le N$ satisfying $z_l-z_j<c(l-j)$.
  - Explanation: A total shortfall bounds the number of endpoints admitting a deficient forward or backward interval.
- **C04** (theorem, [source](#stmt-can-theorem)): There are constants $\delta>0$, $C<\infty$, $\gamma>0$, and $M_0<\infty$ such that, for every $M\ge M_0$, every $i\in\mathbb{Z}$, and every $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $$\label{can:event-bound} \mathbb{P}\left\{ \begin{array}{l} \min\bigl(\log_M\left\lVert T_{i,i-n}\right\rVert, \log_M\left\lVert T_{i,i+n}\right\rVert\bigr)\ge(1-\delta)n,\\[2pt] \log_M\left\lVert T_{i-n,i+n}\right\rVert\le\delta n \end{array} \right\} \le C\exp(-\gamma n).$$ All four constants are independent of the center, the length, and the parameter $M$ in the indicated range.
  - Explanation: Near-maximal growth on both wings and near-total cancellation across their concatenation have exponentially small area probability.
- **C06** (bound, [source](#stmt-can-many-good)): There is a constant $A$ such that, on each outward path associated to <a href="#can:small-solution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="can:small-solution">[can:small-solution]</a>, the number of indices in $\{2,\ldots,N-2\}$ which are not good is at most $$\label{can:bad-count} \frac{A(\delta n+1)}{1-c}.$$ The estimate holds uniformly for $M\ge e$.
  - Explanation: Small endpoint values force most outward indices to satisfy the prescribed expansion and decay inequalities.
- **C07** (bound, [source](#stmt-can-matching)): There are fixed constants $\delta_0>0$, $c_1>0$, $C_1<\infty$, and $n_*<\infty$ with the following property. If $0<\delta\le\delta_0$ is fixed, increasing $n_*$ if necessary, every configuration in <a href="#can:small-solution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="can:small-solution">[can:small-solution]</a> with $n\ge n_*$ has at least $c_1n$ pairs $(j_m^+,j_m^-)$ of good indices such that both index sequences increase strictly and $$\label{can:matching-condition} \left\lvert\tau_{j_m^+}^+-\tau_{j_m^-}^-\right\rvert\le\frac35.$$ The collection of all possible increasing index-pair patterns for a given $n$ has cardinality at most $\exp(C_1n)$.
  - Explanation: A positive fraction of increasing good-index pairs have comparable logarithmic derivative scales, with exponentially many possible patterns.
- **C08** (bound, [source](#stmt-can-local-scarcity)): For every $\eta>0$ there are $\eta'>0$ and $M_\eta<\infty$ such that the following holds for $M\ge M_\eta$. Fix $q_e$, a base coordinate $\theta_*$, and indices $j_+,j_-\ge2$ on the two outward paths. Suppose both indices are good at $\theta_*$ for a common normalized solution, and their logarithms satisfy <a href="#can:matching-condition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="can:matching-condition">[can:matching-condition]</a>. Let $$L_+=\left\lvert t_{j_+}^+(\theta_*)\right\rvert,\qquad L_-=\left\lvert t_{j_-}^-(\theta_*)\right\rvert,\qquad L=\max(L_+,L_-).$$ Let $\mathcal S\subset[-1,1]$ consist of the $h$ for which, at $\theta=\theta_*+h/L$, the same two indices are good for some common solution with $u_e=1$. The solution is allowed to depend on $h$, and neither endpoint smallness nor a new matching condition is required at these other points. Then $$\label{can:scarcity-bound} \left\lvert\{x\in\mathbb{R}:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,\mathcal S)<\eta'\}\right\rvert\le\eta.$$ Here and below the size of a subset of the line or circle denotes its Lebesgue measure. The constants are uniform over the central position, the base coordinate, and the selected indices.
  - Explanation: Matched good indices can recur only on a uniformly small neighborhood of successful rescaled coordinates, including degenerate configurations.
- **C09** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bridge-theorem)): There are absolute constants $c_b\in(0,1)$, $\kappa>0$, and $\sigma>0$ with the following property. Fix observation bounds $(R,L,B)$ and a threshold $h>0$. For sufficiently large $M$ and sufficiently large $n=b-a$, let $W,H$ be observations with these bounds, with $W\geq0$. All indices occurring in one observation are within $\kappa n$ of $a$, and all indices occurring in the other are within $\kappa n$ of $b$. Either assignment of $W,H$ to the two ends is allowed. Then $$\mathbb{E}\!\left[ \mathbf{1}_{\{\log_M\left\lVert T_{a,b}\right\rVert\geq c_b n\}} W\mathbf{1}_{\{H>h\}} \right] \leq C\bigl(\mathbb{E}W+M^{-\sigma n}\bigr)\mathbb{P}(H>h/2). \label{bridge:product}$$ The constants $c_b,\kappa,\sigma$ are independent of the observation bounds and threshold. The constant $C$ and the lower size requirements for $M,n$ may depend on $(R,L,B)$ and $h$, but not on the indices, the defining functions, or the bridge location.
  - Explanation: Observations at opposite ends admit a weighted product bound on the fast-bridge event, without an independence assumption.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-bridge-graphs)): For sufficiently large $M$, suppose $B_+\geq2$ and, at a fixed state with central coordinates $(x,y)=(q_0,q_{-1})$, $$D_l:=\left\lVert T_{0,l}\right\rVert\geq M^{cl} \qquad(1\leq l\leq B_+). \label{bridge:prefix-growth}$$ For at least one choice $j\in\{B_+-1,B_+\}$ there is a smooth graph through this state, parametrized by $q_{-1}=y+r$, $\left\lvert r\right\rvert\leq1/4$, on which the lifted coordinate $q_j$ is fixed. On this graph, $$\left\lvert q_p(r)-q_p(0)\right\rvert+ \left\lvert\frac{\,\mathrm{d}q_p(r)}{\,\mathrm{d}r}\right\rvert \leq C M^{-0.8(p+1)} \qquad(0\leq p<j). \label{bridge:graph-contraction}$$ The corresponding endpoint pair at time $B_+$ changes by at most $C M^{-0.8(B_+-1)}$. The partial derivative $\partial q_j/\partial q_0$, with $q_{-1}$ held independent, is nonzero throughout the graph, and the absolute values of this partial derivative at any two points of the graph have a ratio between two absolute positive constants.* *For a fixed choice of $j$, one may require the closed eligibility condition $$\left\lvert\partial q_j/\partial q_0\right\rvert\geq D_{B_+}/3 \quad\text{at the initial point}. \label{bridge:row-choice}$$ The graphs depend continuously on eligible initial points. There is an identical backward statement, with almost horizontal graphs in $(x,y)$, obtained by using $(q_{-1},q_0)$ as the ordered initial pair.
  - Explanation: Finite Dirichlet problems yield contracting graph families with uniform transverse distortion under prefix growth.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-scale-fourpoint)): Fix a base index $i$ and write $$D_j=\left\lVert T_{i,j}\right\rVert,\qquad R_{jp}=M^{g(j,p)-g(i,j)-g(i,p)}.$$ There is an absolute constant $C_0\ge1$ such that, for every orbit, every sufficiently large parameter, and every choice of integer indices, $$\label{scale:R-fourpoint} R_{jp}\le C_0\max\{R_{jl},R_{lp}\}.$$
  - Explanation: Transfer growth ratios satisfy a uniform four-point comparison derived from determinant-one singular directions.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-scale-truncation)): Fix integers $i$, $n\ge1$, and $1\le s\le n$, and put $$L=i-n,\quad R=i+n,\quad L'=i-s,\quad R'=i+s,$$ $$D=n-\min\{g(i,L),g(i,R)\},\qquad \ell=g(i,L)+g(i,R)-g(L,R).$$ Use the quantities $R_{uv}$ of <a href="#scale:fourpoint" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="scale:fourpoint">11</a> with base $i$. Then $$\begin{aligned} g(i,L'),g(i,R')&\ge s-D, \label{scale:cut-wings}\\ R_{L'R'}&\le C_0^2\max\{R_{LR},M^{-2s+2D}\}. \label{scale:cut-R} \end{aligned}$$ There is a constant $A$ independent of the parameter and indices such that the cut cancellation loss satisfies $$\label{scale:cut-loss} g(i,L')+g(i,R')-g(L',R') \ge\min\{\ell,2s-2D\}-A,$$ and consequently $$\label{scale:cut-full} g(L',R')\le2s-\min\{\ell,2s-2D\}+A.$$
  - Explanation: Cutting a cancellation retains a controlled portion of its loss with a uniform additive error.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-scale-doubling)): For all sufficiently large parameters and every integer $n\ge1$, $$\label{scale:doubling-equation} e_n\le e_{2n}\le C e_n+\frac{C}{n},$$ with a constant independent of $n$ and the parameter.
  - Explanation: Expected shortfall is nondecreasing under doubling and has an upper bound controlled by the preceding scale.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-scale-selection)): Along the assumed zero-exponent parameter sequence, there are powers of two $n_0=n_0(K)$ and $N=N(K)>n_0$, and numbers $\epsilon=e_N$, such that $$\label{scale:selected-limits} n_0\to\infty,\qquad \epsilon\to0,\qquad \epsilon n_0\to\infty,$$ and, for every fixed integer $m\in\mathbb{Z}$ (once the lengths below are positive integers), $$\label{scale:selected-boundaries} e_{2^m n_0}=o(\epsilon),\qquad e_{2^m N}=O_m(\epsilon).$$ All scale sums below run over powers of two.
  - Explanation: Under the assumed zero-exponent parameter sequence, critical dyadic scales separate negligible initial deficit from a small terminal deficit.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-scale-local-mass)): Let $I$ have dyadic endpoints and length $p=2^m$, where $m\in\mathbb{Z}$ is fixed. Then $$\label{scale:sum-test-bound} \int\bigl(J(I)+V(I)\bigr)\,\,\mathrm{d}\mu_K=O_I(1).$$ Uniformly over the individual dyadic scales $n_0\le n\le N$, $$\label{scale:individual-test-bound} \epsilon^{-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[h(I)(g_n)+J(I)(g_n)+V(I)(g_n)\bigr] =O_I(1).$$ At the initial scale the stronger bound holds: $$\label{scale:initial-test-bound} \epsilon^{-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[h(I)(g_{n_0})+J(I)(g_{n_0}) +V(I)(g_{n_0})\bigr]=o(1).$$ All three statements concern sufficiently large $K$, so that the indicated endpoints and half-endpoints align.
  - Explanation: Local jump and variance tests have bounded cumulative rescaled mass and negligible initial-scale mass.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-scale-affinity-tests)): As $I$ ranges over intervals with dyadic endpoints and power-of-two length, the common zero set of $J(I)+V(I)$ is exactly $\mathcal A$. Consequently, every compact subset $F\subset\mathcal U$ admits finitely many such intervals $I_1,\ldots,I_b$ and a number $c_F>0$ such that $$\label{scale:finite-test-cover} \sum_{a=1}^{b}\bigl(J(I_a)+V(I_a)\bigr)\ge c_F \quad\hbox{on }F.$$
  - Explanation: Vanishing of all dyadic jump and variance tests characterizes affinity; finitely many tests detect compact sets away from it.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-scale-limits)): After passing to a subsequence, the restrictions of $\mu_K$ and $\nu_K$ to $\mathcal U$ converge vaguely to locally finite nonnegative Borel measures $\mu$ and $\nu$, respectively. Moreover, $$\label{scale:initial-negligible} \xi_K\longrightarrow0\quad\hbox{vaguely on }\mathcal U.$$ The measures $\mu$ and $\nu$ are supported on the closed class $X_0\subset X$ consisting of arrays satisfying $d(s,t)\le\|s-t\|$ and the inequalities $$\label{scale:tree-inequality} B_v(s,t)\ge\min\{B_v(s,p),B_v(p,t)\},\qquad B_v(s,t)=d(v,s)+d(v,t)-d(s,t)$$ for every $v,s,t,p\in\mathcal D$. Every member of $X_0$ extends uniquely to a continuous semimetric on $\mathbb{R}$, obeying the same inequalities at all real arguments. Both limiting measures are invariant under $$(\tau_r d)(s,t)=d(s+r,t+r)\qquad(r\in\mathcal D).$$ For every interval $I$ as in <a href="#scale:local-mass" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="scale:local-mass">15</a>, $$\label{scale:limiting-test-bound} \int(J(I)+V(I))\,\,\mathrm{d}\mu<\infty, \qquad \int(h(I)+J(I)+V(I))\,\,\mathrm{d}\nu<\infty.$$ In particular, $$\label{scale:terminal-shortfall} \int h([0,1])\,\,\mathrm{d}\nu\le1.$$
  - Explanation: Locally finite vague limits away from affinity obey translation invariance and continuous semimetric support restrictions.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-scale-decomposition)): On $\mathcal U$, with the measures supported on $X_0$ as above, $$\label{scale:measure-balance} S_*\mu=\mu+\nu.$$ There is a locally finite nonnegative measure $\mu_\infty$, invariant under $S$ and every dyadic translation, such that $$\label{scale:measure-decomposition} \mu=\mu_\infty+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(S^{-j})_*\nu, \qquad S_*\mu_\infty=\mu_\infty.$$ The series is a series of positive measures dominated by $\mu$.
  - Explanation: Dilation gives a positive decomposition into backward terminal dilates and a translation- and dilation-invariant remainder.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-shape-exclusions)): There is a measurable set of full measure for $\mu$, $\nu$, and $\mu_\infty$ on which the following statements hold for every real choice of their arguments.* 1. *There do not exist $r\in\mathbb{R}$ and $t>0$ such that $$\label{shape:forbidden-triple} d(r-t,r)>(1-\delta)t,\qquad d(r,r+t)>(1-\delta)t,\qquad d(r-t,r+t)<\delta t.$$* 2. *If $s\in P_d$ and $t\in Q_d$, then $$\label{shape:slow-pair} d(s,t)\le c_b\|t-s\|.$$* *The same statements hold almost everywhere for every positive measure appearing in the decomposition of <a href="#scale:decomposition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="scale:decomposition">18</a>.
  - Explanation: The limiting support excludes strong cancellation triples and bounds distances between nonaffine and non-unit neighborhoods.
- **C20** (classification, [source](#stmt-shape-classification)): Every nonaffine array in $X_0$ satisfying <a href="#shape:exclusions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shape:exclusions">19</a> has one of the following forms:* 1. *every secant has speed at most $c_b$;* 2. *there is one exterior ray of exact unit speed, and the complementary closed half-line has all secant speeds at most $c_b$;* 3. *there are two exterior rays of exact unit speed, separated by a closed interval $[a,b]$ of positive length, and every secant contained in $[a,b]$ has speed at most $c_b$.* *The second possibility includes either orientation. These descriptions do not assert additivity of distances across the slow part. The three temporal configurations are illustrated in <a href="#shape:figure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shape:figure">3</a>.
  - Explanation: Nonaffine supported arrays consist of a slow region with possibly exterior unit-speed rays, without requiring additivity across that region.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-shape-crossloss)): Suppose there are two exterior unit rays with slow interval $[a,b]$, and put $L=b-a$. Then $L>0$, and, with the fixed constant $$\label{shape:cross-constant} C_*=2+\frac6\delta,$$ every $z\in\mathbb{R}$ and $t>0$ satisfy $$\label{shape:cross-bound} 0\le 1-\frac{d(z,z+t)}t\le\frac{C_*L}{t}.$$ The shortfall is zero whenever the interval is wholly in either closed unit ray.
  - Explanation: The slow interval between exterior unit rays has positive length and bounds the total crossing shortfall.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-end-balance)): With the preceding choices, $$\label{end:balance-equation} \int G\,\,\mathrm{d}\mu=H_\Phi>0.$$
  - Explanation: Dyadic telescoping survives the limiting process without loss of weighted mass to affine arrays.
- **C23** (bound, [source](#stmt-end-terminal)): The series in <a href="#end:integrated-decomposition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="end:integrated-decomposition">[end:integrated-decomposition]</a> satisfies $$\label{end:terminal-strict} \sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int G(S^{-j}d)\,\,\mathrm{d}\nu(d)<H_\Phi.$$
  - Explanation: The backward terminal series is strictly below the positive terminal balance because slow intervals remain visible at fine scales.
- **C24** (bound, [source](#stmt-end-remainder)): The invariant remainder satisfies $$\label{end:remainder-bound} \int G\,\,\mathrm{d}\mu_\infty\le0.$$
  - Explanation: Translation and dilation invariance force a nonpositive capped-deficit contribution from the remainder.
- **C25** (theorem, [source](#stmt-end-contradiction)): There is no sequence $k\to+\infty$ along which the largest Lyapunov exponent of $f_k$ is zero almost everywhere. Consequently <a href="#main:entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:entropy">1</a> holds.
  - Explanation: The terminal and remainder bounds contradict the limiting balance, excluding arbitrarily large zero-exponent parameters.

      "exact_statement": "There is a constant $A$ such that, on each outward path associated to <a href=\"#can:small-solution\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"can:small-solution\">[can:small-solution]</a>, the number of indices in $\\{2,\\ldots,N-2\\}$ which are not good is at most $$\\label{can:bad-count}\n\\frac{A(\\delta n+1)}{1-c}.$$ The estimate holds uniformly for $M\\ge e$.",
      "explanation": "Small endpoint values force most outward indices to satisfy the prescribed expansion and decay inequalities.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-can-matching",
        "exact_quote": "There are fixed constants $\\delta_0>0$, $c_1>0$, $C_1<\\infty$, and $n_*<\\infty$ with the following property. If $0<\\delta\\le\\delta_0$ is fixed, increasing $n_*$ if necessary, every configuration in <a href=\"#can:small-solution\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"can:small-solution\">[can:small-solution]</a> with $n\\ge n_*$ has at least $c_1n$ pairs $(j_m^+,j_m^-)$ of good indices such that both index sequences increase strictly and $$\\label{can:matching-condition}\n\\left\\lvert\\tau_{j_m^+}^+-\\tau_{j_m^-}^-\\right\\rvert\\le\\frac35.$$ The collection of all possible increasing index-pair patterns for a given $n$ has cardinality at most $\\exp(C_1n)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-146.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-can-matching",

          "source_id": "PM-146.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-can-local-scarcity",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\eta>0$ there are $\\eta'>0$ and $M_\\eta<\\infty$ such that the following holds for $M\\ge M_\\eta$. Fix $q_e$, a base coordinate $\\theta_*$, and indices $j_+,j_-\\ge2$ on the two outward paths. Suppose both indices are good at $\\theta_*$ for a common normalized solution, and their logarithms satisfy <a href=\"#can:matching-condition\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"can:matching-condition\">[can:matching-condition]</a>. Let $$L_+=\\left\\lvert t_{j_+}^+(\\theta_*)\\right\\rvert,\\qquad\nL_-=\\left\\lvert t_{j_-}^-(\\theta_*)\\right\\rvert,\\qquad L=\\max(L_+,L_-).$$ Let $\\mathcal S\\subset[-1,1]$ consist of the $h$ for which, at $\\theta=\\theta_*+h/L$, the same two indices are good for some common solution with $u_e=1$. The solution is allowed to depend on $h$, and neither endpoint smallness nor a new matching condition is required at these other points. Then $$\\label{can:scarcity-bound}\n\\left\\lvert\\{x\\in\\mathbb{R}:\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,\\mathcal S)<\\eta'\\}\\right\\rvert\\le\\eta.$$ Here and below the size of a subset of the line or circle denotes its Lebesgue measure. The constants are uniform over the central position, the base coordinate, and the selected indices.",
        "source_id": "PM-146.1"
      },

        {

Let $$L_+=\\left\\lvert t_{j_+}^+(\\theta_*)\\right\\rvert,\\qquad\nL_-=\\left\\lvert t_{j_-}^-(\\theta_*)\\right\\rvert,\\qquad L=\\max(L_+,L_-).$$ Let $\\mathcal S\\subset[-1,1]$ consist of the $h$ for which, at $\\theta=\\theta_*+h/L$, the same two indices are good for some common solution with $u_e=1$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bridge-theorem",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c_b\\in(0,1)$, $\\kappa>0$, and $\\sigma>0$ with the following property. Fix observation bounds $(R,L,B)$ and a threshold $h>0$. For sufficiently large $M$ and sufficiently large $n=b-a$, let $W,H$ be observations with these bounds, with $W\\geq0$. All indices occurring in one observation are within $\\kappa n$ of $a$, and all indices occurring in the other are within $\\kappa n$ of $b$. Either assignment of $W,H$ to the two ends is allowed. Then $$\\mathbb{E}\\!\\left[\n   \\mathbf{1}_{\\{\\log_M\\left\\lVert T_{a,b}\\right\\rVert\\geq c_b n\\}}\n   W\\mathbf{1}_{\\{H>h\\}}\n \\right]\n \\leq\n C\\bigl(\\mathbb{E}W+M^{-\\sigma n}\\bigr)\\mathbb{P}(H>h/2).\n \\label{bridge:product}$$ The constants $c_b,\\kappa,\\sigma$ are independent of the observation bounds and threshold. The constant $C$ and the lower size requirements for $M,n$ may depend on $(R,L,B)$ and $h$, but not on the indices, the defining functions, or the bridge location.",
        "source_id": "PM-146.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bridge-theorem",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c_b\\in(0,1)$, $\\kappa>0$, and $\\sigma>0$ with the following property. Fix observation bounds $(R,L,B)$ and a threshold $h>0$. For sufficiently large $M$ and sufficiently large $n=b-a$, let $W,H$ be observations with these bounds, with $W\\geq0$. All indices occurring in one observation are within $\\kappa n$ of $a$, and all indices occurring in the other are within $\\kappa n$ of $b$. Either assignment of $W,H$ to the two ends is allowed. Then $$\\mathbb{E}\\!\\left[\n   \\mathbf{1}_{\\{\\log_M\\left\\lVert T_{a,b}\\right\\rVert\\geq c_b n\\}}\n   W\\mathbf{1}_{\\{H>h\\}}\n \\right]\n \\leq\n C\\bigl(\\mathbb{E}W+M^{-\\sigma n}\\bigr)\\mathbb{P}(H>h/2).\n \\label{bridge:product}$$ The constants $c_b,\\kappa,\\sigma$ are independent of the observation bounds and threshold. The constant $C$ and the lower size requirements for $M,n$ may depend on $(R,L,B)$ and $h$, but not on the indices, the defining functions, or the bridge location.",
          "source_id": "PM-146.1"
        }
      ],

The constant $C$ and the lower size requirements for $M,n$ may depend on $(R,L,B)$ and $h$, but not on the indices, the defining functions, or the bridge location.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bridge-graphs",
        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large $M$, suppose $B_+\\geq2$ and, at a fixed state with central coordinates $(x,y)=(q_0,q_{-1})$, $$D_l:=\\left\\lVert T_{0,l}\\right\\rVert\\geq M^{cl}\n \\qquad(1\\leq l\\leq B_+).\n \\label{bridge:prefix-growth}$$ For at least one choice $j\\in\\{B_+-1,B_+\\}$ there is a smooth graph through this state, parametrized by $q_{-1}=y+r$, $\\left\\lvert r\\right\\rvert\\leq1/4$, on which the lifted coordinate $q_j$ is fixed. On this graph, $$\\left\\lvert q_p(r)-q_p(0)\\right\\rvert+\n \\left\\lvert\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}q_p(r)}{\\,\\mathrm{d}r}\\right\\rvert\n \\leq C M^{-0.8(p+1)}\n \\qquad(0\\leq p<j).\n \\label{bridge:graph-contraction}$$ The corresponding endpoint pair at time $B_+$ changes by at most $C M^{-0.8(B_+-1)}$. The partial derivative $\\partial q_j/\\partial q_0$, with $q_{-1}$ held independent, is nonzero throughout the graph, and the absolute values of this partial derivative at any two points of the graph have a ratio between two absolute positive constants.*\n\n\n*For a fixed choice of $j$, one may require the closed eligibility condition $$\\left\\lvert\\partial q_j/\\partial q_0\\right\\rvert\\geq D_{B_+}/3\n \\quad\\text{at the initial point}.\n \\label{bridge:row-choice}$$ The graphs depend continuously on eligible initial points. There is an identical backward statement, with almost horizontal graphs in $(x,y)$, obtained by using $(q_{-1},q_0)$ as the ordered initial pair.",
        "source_id": "PM-146.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bridge-graphs",
          "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large $M$, suppose $B_+\\geq2$ and, at a fixed state with central coordinates $(x,y)=(q_0,q_{-1})$, $$D_l:=\\left\\lVert T_{0,l}\\right\\rVert\\geq M^{cl}\n \\qquad(1\\leq l\\leq B_+).\n \\label{bridge:prefix-growth}$$ For at least one choice $j\\in\\{B_+-1,B_+\\}$ there is a smooth graph through this state, parametrized by $q_{-1}=y+r$, $\\left\\lvert r\\right\\rvert\\leq1/4$, on which the lifted coordinate $q_j$ is fixed. On this graph, $$\\left\\lvert q_p(r)-q_p(0)\\right\\rvert+\n \\left\\lvert\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}q_p(r)}{\\,\\mathrm{d}r}\\right\\rvert\n \\leq C M^{-0.8(p+1)}\n \\qquad(0\\leq p<j).\n \\label{bridge:graph-contraction}$$ The corresponding endpoint pair at time $B_+$ changes by at most $C M^{-0.8(B_+-1)}$. The partial derivative $\\partial q_j/\\partial q_0$, with $q_{-1}$ held independent, is nonzero throughout the graph, and the absolute values of this partial derivative at any two points of the graph have a ratio between two absolute positive constants.*\n\n\n*For a fixed choice of $j$, one may require the closed eligibility condition $$\\left\\lvert\\partial q_j/\\partial q_0\\right\\rvert\\geq D_{B_+}/3\n \\quad\\text{at the initial point}.\n \\label{bridge:row-choice}$$ The graphs depend continuously on eligible initial points. There is an identical backward statement, with almost horizontal graphs in $(x,y)$, obtained by using $(q_{-1},q_0)$ as the ordered initial pair.",
          "source_id": "PM-146.1"

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bridge-graphs",
          "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large $M$, suppose $B_+\\geq2$ and, at a fixed state with central coordinates $(x,y)=(q_0,q_{-1})$, $$D_l:=\\left\\lVert T_{0,l}\\right\\rVert\\geq M^{cl}\n \\qquad(1\\leq l\\leq B_+).\n \\label{bridge:prefix-growth}$$ For at least one choice $j\\in\\{B_+-1,B_+\\}$ there is a smooth graph through this state, parametrized by $q_{-1}=y+r$, $\\left\\lvert r\\right\\rvert\\leq1/4$, on which the lifted coordinate $q_j$ is fixed. On this graph, $$\\left\\lvert q_p(r)-q_p(0)\\right\\rvert+\n \\left\\lvert\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}q_p(r)}{\\,\\mathrm{d}r}\\right\\rvert\n \\leq C M^{-0.8(p+1)}\n \\qquad(0\\leq p<j).\n \\label{bridge:graph-contraction}$$ The corresponding endpoint pair at time $B_+$ changes by at most $C M^{-0.8(B_+-1)}$. The partial derivative $\\partial q_j/\\partial q_0$, with $q_{-1}$ held independent, is nonzero throughout the graph, and the absolute values of this partial derivative at any two points of the graph have a ratio between two absolute positive constants.*\n\n\n*For a fixed choice of $j$, one may require the closed eligibility condition $$\\left\\lvert\\partial q_j/\\partial q_0\\right\\rvert\\geq D_{B_+}/3\n \\quad\\text{at the initial point}.\n \\label{bridge:row-choice}$$ The graphs depend continuously on eligible initial points. There is an identical backward statement, with almost horizontal graphs in $(x,y)$, obtained by using $(q_{-1},q_0)$ as the ordered initial pair.",
          "source_id": "PM-146.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-bridge-graphs"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-scale-fourpoint",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a base index $i$ and write $$D_j=\\left\\lVert T_{i,j}\\right\\rVert,\\qquad\n R_{jp}=M^{g(j,p)-g(i,j)-g(i,p)}.$$ There is an absolute constant $C_0\\ge1$ such that, for every orbit, every sufficiently large parameter, and every choice of integer indices, $$\\label{scale:R-fourpoint}\n R_{jp}\\le C_0\\max\\{R_{jl},R_{lp}\\}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-146.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-scale-truncation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix integers $i$, $n\\ge1$, and $1\\le s\\le n$, and put $$L=i-n,\\quad R=i+n,\\quad L'=i-s,\\quad R'=i+s,$$ $$D=n-\\min\\{g(i,L),g(i,R)\\},\\qquad\n \\ell=g(i,L)+g(i,R)-g(L,R).$$ Use the quantities $R_{uv}$ of <a href=\"#scale:fourpoint\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"scale:fourpoint\">11</a> with base $i$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-scale-selection",
        "exact_quote": "Along the assumed zero-exponent parameter sequence, there are powers of two $n_0=n_0(K)$ and $N=N(K)>n_0$, and numbers $\\epsilon=e_N$, such that $$\\label{scale:selected-limits}\n n_0\\to\\infty,\\qquad \\epsilon\\to0,\\qquad\n \\epsilon n_0\\to\\infty,$$ and, for every fixed integer $m\\in\\mathbb{Z}$ (once the lengths below are positive integers), $$\\label{scale:selected-boundaries}\n e_{2^m n_0}=o(\\epsilon),\\qquad\n e_{2^m N}=O_m(\\epsilon).$$ All scale sums below run over powers of two.",
        "source_id": "PM-146.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-scale-local-mass",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I$ have dyadic endpoints and length $p=2^m$, where $m\\in\\mathbb{Z}$ is fixed.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-scale-affinity-tests",
        "exact_quote": "As $I$ ranges over intervals with dyadic endpoints and power-of-two length, the common zero set of $J(I)+V(I)$ is exactly $\\mathcal A$. Consequently, every compact subset $F\\subset\\mathcal U$ admits finitely many such intervals $I_1,\\ldots,I_b$ and a number $c_F>0$ such that $$\\label{scale:finite-test-cover}\n \\sum_{a=1}^{b}\\bigl(J(I_a)+V(I_a)\\bigr)\\ge c_F\n \\quad\\hbox{on }F.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-146.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-scale-affinity-tests",

          "source_id": "PM-146.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-scale-limits",
        "exact_quote": "After passing to a subsequence, the restrictions of $\\mu_K$ and $\\nu_K$ to $\\mathcal U$ converge vaguely to locally finite nonnegative Borel measures $\\mu$ and $\\nu$, respectively. Moreover, $$\\label{scale:initial-negligible}\n \\xi_K\\longrightarrow0\\quad\\hbox{vaguely on }\\mathcal U.$$ The measures $\\mu$ and $\\nu$ are supported on the closed class $X_0\\subset X$ consisting of arrays satisfying $d(s,t)\\le|s-t|$ and the inequalities $$\\label{scale:tree-inequality}\n B_v(s,t)\\ge\\min\\{B_v(s,p),B_v(p,t)\\},\\qquad\n B_v(s,t)=d(v,s)+d(v,t)-d(s,t)$$ for every $v,s,t,p\\in\\mathcal D$. Every member of $X_0$ extends uniquely to a continuous semimetric on $\\mathbb{R}$, obeying the same inequalities at all real arguments. Both limiting measures are invariant under $$(\\tau_r d)(s,t)=d(s+r,t+r)\\qquad(r\\in\\mathcal D).$$ For every interval $I$ as in <a href=\"#scale:local-mass\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"scale:local-mass\">15</a>, $$\\label{scale:limiting-test-bound}\n \\int(J(I)+V(I))\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}\\mu<\\infty,\n \\qquad\n \\int(h(I)+J(I)+V(I))\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}\\nu<\\infty.$$ In particular, $$\\label{scale:terminal-shortfall}\n \\int h([0,1])\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}\\nu\\le1.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-scale-decomposition",
        "exact_quote": "On $\\mathcal U$, with the measures supported on $X_0$ as above, $$\\label{scale:measure-balance}\n S_*\\mu=\\mu+\\nu.$$ There is a locally finite nonnegative measure $\\mu_\\infty$, invariant under $S$ and every dyadic translation, such that $$\\label{scale:measure-decomposition}\n \\mu=\\mu_\\infty+\\sum_{j=1}^{\\infty}(S^{-j})_*\\nu,\n \\qquad S_*\\mu_\\infty=\\mu_\\infty.$$ The series is a series of positive measures dominated by $\\mu$.",
        "source_id": "PM-146.1"
      },

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-shape-exclusions",
        "exact_quote": "There is a measurable set of full measure for $\\mu$, $\\nu$, and $\\mu_\\infty$ on which the following statements hold for every real choice of their arguments.*\n\n\n1.  *There do not exist $r\\in\\mathbb{R}$ and $t>0$ such that $$\\label{shape:forbidden-triple}\n     d(r-t,r)>(1-\\delta)t,\\qquad\n     d(r,r+t)>(1-\\delta)t,\\qquad\n     d(r-t,r+t)<\\delta t.$$*\n\n\n2.  *If $s\\in P_d$ and $t\\in Q_d$, then $$\\label{shape:slow-pair}\n     d(s,t)\\le c_b|t-s|.$$*\n\n*The same statements hold almost everywhere for every positive measure appearing in the decomposition of <a href=\"#scale:decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"scale:decomposition\">18</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-146.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-shape-classification",
        "exact_quote": "Every nonaffine array in $X_0$ satisfying <a href=\"#shape:exclusions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"shape:exclusions\">19</a> has one of the following forms:*\n\n1.  *every secant has speed at most $c_b$;*\n\n2.  *there is one exterior ray of exact unit speed, and the complementary closed half-line has all secant speeds at most $c_b$;*\n\n3.  *there are two exterior rays of exact unit speed, separated by a closed interval $[a,b]$ of positive length, and every secant contained in $[a,b]$ has speed at most $c_b$.*\n\n*The second possibility includes either orientation.         "anchor": "stmt-shape-crossloss",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose there are two exterior unit rays with slow interval $[a,b]$, and put $L=b-a$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Standard sine map** ([source](#main:map)): The standard sine map is the area-preserving diffeomorphism $$\label{main:map} f_k(x,y)=\bigl(x+y+k\sin(2\pi x),\;y+k\sin(2\pi x)\bigr) \pmod{\mathbb{Z}^2},\qquad k\in\mathbb{R}.$$
- **Transfer matrices** ([source](#setup:transfer)): For $a,b\in\mathbb{Z}$, let $T_{a,b}$ carry the linearized pair at time $a$ to the pair at time $b$.
- **Growth and expected shortfall** ([source](#scale:distance-deficit)): We record growth and its expected shortfall by $$\label{scale:distance-deficit} g(i,j)=\log_M\left\lVert T_{i,j}\right\rVert,\qquad e_n=\mathbb{E}\left[1-\frac{g(0,n)}n\right]\quad(n\ge1).$$
- **Good index** ([source](#stmt-can-good-definition)): An index $2\le j\le N-2$ is *good* for the selected normalized solution if $$\label{can:good} \begin{aligned} \tau_j-\tau_l&\ge c(j-l)&& (1\le l<j),\\ \tau_{j+1}-\tau_j&\ge c,\\ \upsilon_j-\upsilon_{j+1}&\ge c, &\upsilon_{j+1}-\upsilon_{j+2}&\ge c. \end{aligned}$$ For a path extending beyond a selected index, the same inequalities define goodness without reference to a particular terminal length $N$.
- **Bridge shortfall** ([source](#bridge:shortfalls)): For distinct integers $d,e$, write $$\mathfrak h_{d,e} =1-\frac{\log_M\left\lVert T_{d,e}\right\rVert}{\left\lvert d-e\right\rvert}. \label{bridge:shortfalls}$$
- **Rescaled distance array** ([source](#scale:rescaled-array)): For a positive integer $n$, define the random array $$\label{scale:rescaled-array} g_n(s,t)=\frac{g(\lfloor ns\rfloor,\lfloor nt\rfloor)}{n} \qquad(s,t\in\mathcal D).$$
- **Capped deficit observable** ([source](#end:G-definition)): Define $$\label{end:G-definition} G(d)=\Phi(h_2(0))-\tfrac12\Phi(h_1(0))-\tfrac12\Phi(h_1(1)).$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R01** [claim](#stmt-setup-shortfall-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-shortfall-count): Disjoint witnessing intervals and enlarged covers. -> A count of deficient endpoints.
- **R02** [claim](#stmt-can-local-scarcity) / [proof](#loc:section): Rescaled derivative bounds, polynomial extraction, and analytic identity obstructions. -> Uniform local scarcity.
- **R03** [claim](#stmt-can-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-can-local-scarcity): Matched-scale coverings and exponential contraction of successful neighborhoods. -> Exponential cancellation probability.
- **R04** [claim](#stmt-bridge-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bridge-graphs-2): Contracting Dirichlet graphs and transverse changes of variables. -> A weighted product estimate on fast bridges.
- **R05** [claim](#stmt-scale-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-scale-selection): Four-point comparison, cancellation truncation, and first-crossing dyadic selection. -> Critical initial and terminal scales.
- **R06** [claim](#stmt-scale-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-scale-limits): Local test bounds, compact cutoffs, and diagonal weak-limit extraction. -> Translation-invariant locally finite limiting measures.
- **R07** [claim](#stmt-scale-decomposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-scale-decomposition): Positive measure iteration under inverse dilation. -> Terminal dilates and an invariant nonnegative remainder.
- **R08** [claim](#stmt-shape-classification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shape-classification): Support exclusions and propagation of local affine speeds by the tree inequality. -> Slow regions with possible exterior unit rays.
- **R09** [claim](#stmt-end-balance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-balance): Dyadic telescoping and separate control near each range of affine speeds. -> An exact positive limiting balance.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-end-terminal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-terminal): Terminal telescoping and a positive fine-scale slow contribution via Fatou’s lemma. -> A strict bound on the terminal series.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-end-remainder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-remainder): Interface intensity and fine-versus-coarse averaging on dilation bands. -> A nonpositive invariant remainder contribution.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-end-contradiction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-contradiction): Combine the terminal and remainder inequalities against the exact balance. -> Eventual positive metric entropy for normalized area.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I_SHORTFALL**: The counts of backward and forward growth violations are controlled by the total nonnegative step shortfall.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A_ENTROPY**: Use finite shortfall counts to certify positive metric entropy. -> A finite sequence test cannot establish the asymptotic entropy assertion.
- **A_INDEPENDENCE**: Treat stationary orbit observations as independent samples. -> Independent sampling can replace the deterministic orbit law and invalidate its probability estimates.
- **A_RATE**: Infer a uniform positive entropy lower bound or full-area hyperbolicity. -> The conclusion can hold on a positive-area subset without the stronger guarantees.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The shortfall-count lemma has a finite sequence input, explicit witnessing inequalities and a constructive covering bound. The finite kernel checks this lemma rather than the entropy conclusion.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of at most 1000 cases; each has rational strings z and c.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= len(z) <= 65, c < 1, successive increments <= 1.
# OUTPUTS: JSON rows [deficit, backward bad count, forward bad count, bound].
# TIME: Linear rational operations in the total sequence length.
# SPACE: Linear in sequence length, excluding rational bit complexity.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact arbitrary-precision rational arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; no approximate result is returned.
import json,sys
from flint import fmpq
cases=json.load(sys.stdin)
if not isinstance(cases,list) or len(cases)>1000: raise ValueError('case limit')
rows=[]
for case in cases:
    z=[fmpq(x) for x in case['z']]; c=fmpq(case['c'])
    if not 1<=len(z)<=65 or c>=1: raise ValueError('regime')
    if any(z[j]-z[j-1]>1 for j in range(1,len(z))): raise ValueError('increment')
    a=[v-c*j for j,v in enumerate(z)]
    high=a[0]; backward=0
    for value in a[1:]:
        backward += int(value < high)
        high=max(high,value)
    low=a[-1]; forward=0
    for value in reversed(a[:-1]):
        forward += int(value > low)
        low=min(low,value)
    deficit=len(z)-1-(z[-1]-z[0])
    bound=4*deficit/(1-c)
    rows.append([str(deficit),backward,forward,str(bound)])
print(json.dumps(rows,separators=(',',':')))
```

        "observed_exit": 6,
        "receipt_sha256": "f2747c8c2c96b904506b2bf9150d6016af88a75e40fe5c5d6674810beff9756c"
      },
      {
        "expected_failure": "Deficit and bound disagree with exact telescoping.",
        "id": "M_DEFICIT",
        "mutation": "Add endpoint growth instead of subtracting it.",
        "observed_exit": 6,
        "receipt_sha256": "93c8ffe26b7e4e8201b03d0155cab7722daa32441b53e81d4f92732fd345776a"
      }
    ],
    "oracle_code": "import json,sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\ntry:\n    cases=json.load(open(sys.argv[1])); observed=json.load(open(sys.argv[2])); declared=json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n    truth=[]\n    for item in cases:\n        points=list(map(Fraction,item['z'])); slope=Fraction(item['c']); n=len(points)-1\n        if slope>=1 or any(points[b]-points[b-1]>1 for b in range(1,n+1)): sys.exit(8)\n        left=sum(any(points[b]-points[a]<slope*(b-a) for a in range(b)) for b in range(n+1))\n        right=sum(any(points[b]-points[a]<slope*(b-a) for b in range(a+1,n+1)) for a in range(n+1))\n        loss=sum(1-points[b]+points[b-1] for b in range(1,n+1))\n        ceiling=Fraction(4)*loss/(1-slope)\n        if loss<0 or left>ceiling or right>ceiling: sys.exit(9)\n        truth.append([str(loss),left,right,str(ceiling)])\n    sys.exit(0 if observed==truth and declared==truth else 6)\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(7)\n",
    "output_contract": "Exact total shortfall, backward and forward bad-index counts, and the covering bound for each supplied sequence.",
    "reason": "The shortfall-count lemma has a finite sequence input, explicit witnessing inequalities and a constructive covering bound. The finite kernel checks this lemma rather than the entropy conclusion.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of at most 1000 cases; each has rational strings z and c.\n# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= len(z) <= 65, c < 1, successive increments <= 1.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON rows [deficit, backward bad count, forward bad count, bound].\n# TIME: Linear rational operations in the total sequence length.\n# SPACE: Linear in sequence length, excluding rational bit complexity.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact arbitrary-precision rational arithmetic.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; no approximate result is returned.\nimport json,sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\ncases=json.load(sys.stdin)\nif not isinstance(cases,list) or len(cases)>1000: raise ValueError('case limit')\nrows=[]\nfor case in cases:\n    z=[fmpq(x) for x in case['z']]; c=fmpq(case['c'])\n    if not 1<=len(z)<=65 or c>=1: raise ValueError('regime')\n    if any(z[j]-z[j-1]>1 for j in range(1,len(z))): raise ValueError('increment')\n    a=[v-c*j for j,v in enumerate(z)]\n    high=a[0]; backward=0\n    for value in a[1:]:\n        backward += int(value < high)\n        high=max(high,value)\n    low=a[-1]; forward=0\n    for value in reversed(a[:-1]):\n        forward += int(value > low)\n        low=min(low,value)\n    deficit=len(z)-1-(z[-1]-z[0])\n    bound=4*deficit/(1-c)\n    rows.append([str(deficit),backward,forward,str(bound)])\nprint(json.dumps(rows,separators=(',',':')))\n",
    "test_vectors": [

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a software-system contract connecting area entropy to reliability, performance, or correctness. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The exact standard sine map provides a conservative dynamics model for exploratory simulation. Its entropy conclusion concerns normalized area and sufficiently large positive parameters; no executable threshold or finite-time certificate is supplied. |
| tools | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | Dyadic jump and variance tests suggest diagnostics for departures from affine distance arrays. The source characterizes affinity using the full family of tests; a finite diagnostic requires a separately justified scope. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The shortfall-count proof gives a finite greedy selection of witnessing intervals under a bounded-increment hypothesis. This suggests an interval-cover diagnostic, while the entropy proof still uses limits and non-explicit size requirements. |
| data_structures | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | Symmetric semimetric arrays provide a representation for transfer-growth comparisons and dyadic local tests. A finite restriction can support diagnostics but omits the full array space and its limiting measures. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: an accelerator architecture, numerical precision contract, or hardware resource bound. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a threat model, unpredictability contract, or cryptographic construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a learning objective, training method, or generalization bound. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: an optimisation problem, decision procedure, or operational objective with certified bounds. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a distributed protocol, consistency model, or fault-tolerance guarantee. |
| other (smooth conservative dynamics) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The stated application is smooth conservative dynamics: the standard sine map has positive metric entropy with respect to normalized area throughout a sufficiently large positive parameter tail. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): The entropy conclusion concerns the exact standard sine map and normalized invariant area. Blocked use: ['Replacing area by an arbitrary invariant measure.']
- **L02** (edge_case): The parameter conclusion is restricted to sufficiently large positive amplitudes. Blocked use: ['Extending the conclusion to every positive amplitude or to negative amplitudes.']
- **L03** (counterexample_scope): The result does not establish ergodicity of the whole torus, almost-everywhere positive exponents, or a uniform positive entropy lower bound. Blocked use: ['Claiming global ergodicity.', 'Claiming positive exponents on almost all area.', 'Claiming a uniform positive entropy lower bound.']
- **L04** (counterexample_scope): Topological complexity and Hausdorff dimension do not establish positive entropy for normalized area. Blocked use: ['Using a hyperbolic set or full dimension as a positive-area certificate.']
- **L05** (hypothesis): Coupling, noise, and smooth perturbations change the system or its probability law. Blocked use: ['Transferring the exact-map conclusion to a perturbed, noisy, or coupled model without a separate argument.']
- **L06** (hypothesis): Stationarity of orbit observations does not imply independence at different times. Blocked use: ['Treating time-separated observations as independent samples.']
- **L07** (hypothesis): The product estimate is restricted to fast bridges and observations satisfying its stated bounds. Blocked use: ['Using unrestricted mixing or an unconditional product law.']
- **L08** (hypothesis): The selected critical scales arise under the assumed zero-exponent parameter sequence. Blocked use: ['Interpreting those scales as an unconditional production scheduling prescription.']
- **L09** (approximation): Vague convergence away from affine arrays alone does not control weighted mass approaching them. Blocked use: ['Dropping the separate control of weighted mass near affine arrays.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The global affinity characterization uses the full family of dyadic interval tests. Blocked use: ['Promoting a finite family of vanishing tests to a global affinity certificate without further hypotheses.']
- **L11** (counterexample_scope): The shape descriptions do not assert additivity across the slow part. Blocked use: ['Replacing the classified semimetric by an additive distance across the slow region.']
- **L12** (nonconstructive): A numerical threshold or computable entropy lower bound is ABSENT from the stated eventual-positivity conclusion. Blocked use: ['Using a supplied numerical amplitude threshold.', 'Certifying positive metric entropy from a finite trajectory calculation.']
- **L13** (unknown): A software reliability, security, or performance contract derived from metric entropy is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Publishing a software guarantee based only on this entropy theorem.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 146 (`lean/docs/146.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Positive Metric Entropy for the Standard Map at Large Parameters](../../preprints/Positive-Metric-Entropy-for-the-Standard-Map-at-Large-Parameters-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves Sinai's positive-entropy conjecture for the standard sine map on the two-dimensional torus in the stronger form of a full positive parameter tail. For every sufficiently large parameter, normalized area has positive metric entropy, and a positive-area set has positive largest Lyapunov exponent with the stated derivative-growth limit.
>
> It also constructs a positive-area invariant ergodic hyperbolic component. That component splits into finitely many cyclic pieces on each of which the corresponding iterate is Bernoulli. No genericity assumption on the parameter is used.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `Circle` | abbrev |
| `Torus` | abbrev |
| `sine_periodic` | lemma |
| `sine` | def |
| `standardMap` | def |
| `area` | def |
| `FinitePartition` | def |
| `blockAtom` | def |
| `blockEntropy` | def |
| `partitionEntropy` | def |
| `metricEntropy` | def |
| `MainObligation` | def |
| `main_entropy` | theorem |

Comparator source `StandardMapEntropy.lean` (1792 bytes, sha256 `5d2a9b57428139199c440e53c836319872f75d7b2788207d710e5d04c49bfd16`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open MeasureTheory Set
open scoped ENNReal BigOperators

namespace StandardMapEntropy

abbrev Circle := AddCircle (1 : ℝ)
abbrev Torus := Circle × Circle

lemma sine_periodic : Function.Periodic (fun x : ℝ => Real.sin (2 * Real.pi * x)) 1 := by
  intro x
  dsimp only
  rw [show 2 * Real.pi * (x + 1) = 2 * Real.pi * x + 2 * Real.pi by ring]
  exact Real.sin_add_two_pi _

noncomputable def sine (x : Circle) : ℝ := sine_periodic.lift x

noncomputable def standardMap (k : ℝ) (z : Torus) : Torus :=
  (z.1 + z.2 + (k * sine z.1 : ℝ), z.2 + (k * sine z.1 : ℝ))

noncomputable def area : Measure Torus :=
  (volume : Measure Circle).prod (volume : Measure Circle)

def FinitePartition (r : ℕ) := { p : Torus → Fin (r + 1) // Measurable p }

def blockAtom {r : ℕ} (f : Torus → Torus) (p : FinitePartition r)
    (n : ℕ) (w : Fin n → Fin (r + 1)) : Set Torus :=
  { z | ∀ i : Fin n, p.val (f^[i.val] z) = w i }

noncomputable def blockEntropy {r : ℕ} (μ : Measure Torus) (f : Torus → Torus)
    (p : FinitePartition r) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  - ∑ w : Fin n → Fin (r + 1),
    (μ (blockAtom f p n w)).toReal * Real.log (μ (blockAtom f p n w)).toReal

noncomputable def partitionEntropy {r : ℕ} (μ : Measure Torus) (f : Torus → Torus)
    (p : FinitePartition r) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨅ n : ℕ, ENNReal.ofReal (blockEntropy μ f p (n + 1) / (n + 1 : ℝ))

noncomputable def metricEntropy (μ : Measure Torus) (f : Torus → Torus) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨆ r : ℕ, ⨆ p : FinitePartition r, partitionEntropy μ f p

def MainObligation : Prop :=
  ∃ k₀ : ℝ, 0 < k₀ ∧ ∀ k : ℝ, k₀ ≤ k → 0 < metricEntropy area (standardMap k)

theorem main_entropy : MainObligation := by
  sorry

end StandardMapEntropy

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the standard sine map of the two-dimensional torus has positive metric entropy with respect to normalized area for every sufficiently large positive parameter. This gives a full parameter tail, and hence answers Sinai's positive-parameter-measure conjecture affirmatively.
